[[page 1]]
Using Primes 26 – 97 fact to make new alphabets from K4 
1 of 66 
The idea stems from trying to find sequences to filter the K4 CT (and later, other texts). 
 
What caught my eye with primes was the fact that there are 26 primes between zero and 97, 
and there are, of course, 26 letters in the modern English alphabet.

At first the goal was to find a 26 letter alphabet (26 unique letters). 
Starting at a T was also employed. 
 
Later, simply determining whether a message or clue(s) might appear in the sequences of 26 filtered letters. 
 
K4 CT 
                          ?OBKR 
UOXOGHULBSOLIFBBWFLRVQQPRNGKSSO 
TWTQSJQSSEKZZWATJKLUDIAWINFBNYP 
VTTMZFPKWGDKZXTJCDIGKUHUAUEKCAR 
 
Later, I use the technique on K1, K2, K2++ and K3 CTs and PTs (with and without the ?’s).
